# libGLcore: the Odyssey driver routines in the other builds

The driver routines of [libglcore-odyssey-reads.md](libglcore-odyssey-reads.md) were read in the N32 build of the 6.5.30 `libGLcore.so` for IP30 (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`). The same names exist in the other builds listed in [docs/inventory/binaries.tsv](inventory/binaries.tsv): the o32 and 64-bit IP30 builds, the IP35 builds and the vpro-patch builds. Every row here gives one routine in one build, at that build's own address and size from the function table ([docs/inventory/functions.tsv](inventory/functions.tsv)). No N32 offset is copied into a row.

A copy is either identical to the N32 routine or it is not.

- **Identical copies** are listed in the first table. For each, the 32-bit instruction words of the copy were taken from the build's own disassembly (`objdump -d`) over the copy's address range and compared word for word with the N32 routine's words over its range. The function table gives the same code hash for both. The N32 routine was read in full, so an identical copy carries the same reading and is **established**. The row does not claim anything about the copy beyond its words matching.
- **Copies that differ** are in the second table, one row per build at that build's own address. Each row gives the body that was read in full and how it differs from the N32 routine, using the copy's own context offsets. Routines not yet in that table still have unread copies in the o32 and 64-bit IP30 builds, the IP35 o32 and 64-bit builds and the vpro-patch builds.

## Identical copies

Words compared: the number of instruction words in the routine, in N32 and in the copy, and how many matched. The function-table hash is the 12-digit code hash the inventory assigns to the routine in that build; it is the same for the N32 routine and for every identical copy.

Notes on individual rows:

- `__glOdysseyValidateClipPlanes`: objdump prints `...` in place of two zero words at 0xd967f38 and 0xd967f3c, in the N32 build and in both copies. Those two words are not in the comparison. The 206 words shown match word for word.
- `MakeCurrentRead` and `DoDomain2WithDerivs` are **inferred** because their N32 entries are: the reads chapter says which parts were read in full. Their copies match the N32 words, so they take the N32 label.
- The copies under `usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32` and `usr/gfx/arch/IP35ODSY32` (the paths without the `builds/6.5.30-media` prefix) call through their own `.got`. The IP35 copy's `.got` is word for word the N32 build's. The IP30 copy's `.got` differs in 36 words, all for imported symbols (dynamic symbol indexes 4283 to 4319, for example `bcopy` at 4300 and `floor` at 4319). The name at every GOT index from 4281 up is the same in the N32 build and in this copy, so each call through a gp slot reaches the same routine.
- The words in these rows were compared on the file bytes at each address, as 32-bit big-endian words, which are the words objdump prints. None of the routines has a direct `j` or `jal`, so no call target is an absolute address that could mean something else in another build.
- The 64-bit copies of `ComputeFirstPartials` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64` at 0x21bb670, and `usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64` and `usr/gfx/arch/IP35ODSY64` at 0x320b670 and 0x21bb670) have the same 39 words as the N32 routine. Its body has no gp-relative load and no call, so the words are the whole comparison.

## Copies that differ

Each row is one routine in one build. "Body read" is the byte range of the routine's code that was disassembled and read in full, which ends at the return. Where a copy's table size runs past its return into the next routine, the table size is given with the body read. A row whose code hash equals the hash of an earlier row in this table reads the same bytes, so it takes that row's reading.

Context offsets are the copy's own. Several builds keep the context record with different field positions (the 64-bit builds use 8-byte pointers in it), so a copy's offsets are not the N32 offsets.

| Routine | Binary | Address | Table size | Body read | Function-table hash | Label | How it differs from the N32 routine |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| `__glOdysseyValidateScissor` | `usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` (builds/6.5.30-media) | 0xd96c430 | 1392 | 360 bytes, 0xd96c430 to 0xd96c598 | dacb097cce56 | Established | Same steps as N32, and the same context offsets (shadow pair +0x7c20 and +0x7c24, saved pair +0x165b4 and +0x165b8, settle word +0x9830, bit-20 word +0x7b70). The routine starts with a PIC prologue that loads gp from t9 and saves gp and ra in a 56-byte frame. The ioctl argument block is copied from a zero-filled template in .data at 0xdaf8060 and each word's low 12 bits are then set to the field, so the four words sent equal the N32 block. The ioctl is called through the GOT slot at gp-30516, which holds 0xd9eb890, the address of `__glOdysseyIoctl` in this build. |
| `__glOdysseyValidateScissor` | `usr/gfx/arch/IP35ODSY/libGLcore.so` | 0xd96c430 | 1392 | same bytes as the IP30 o32 row | dacb097cce56 | Established | Same code hash as the IP30 o32 row above, so the same reading applies. |

Its own offsets: shadow pair +0x9f70 and +0x9f74, saved pair +0x18a24 and +0x18a28, settle word +0xbb80, bit-20 word +0x9ec0. The shadow is updated from the saved pair before the settle test, the settle block sends 0x10443, 250, 0x10046 and 0x10046, and the four ioctl fields are built as in N32. The ioctl is called through the GOT slot at gp-28272, which holds 0x221da60, the address of `__glOdysseyIoctl` in this build. |

## Copies with the same code hash

Each row is one routine in one build whose function-table code hash equals the hash of the N32 routine named in the reads chapter ([libglcore-odyssey-reads.md](libglcore-odyssey-reads.md)). The hash is the first 12 hex digits of the hash of the function's bytes, taken over the table size, and every row here has the same table size as its N32 routine. The inventory computed each hash over that build's own bytes, so an equal hash means equal bytes and the copy carries the N32 reading. The label follows the N32 reading: **Established** when the N32 entry in the reads chapter is established throughout, **Inferred** when part of it is inferred (as in the first table). The matching was done by the inventory script; the copy was not disassembled row by row. One spot check was made by hand: `CheckMMXfrArgs`, 128 bytes at 0xd9c6ca0 in the N32 build and at 0x325b220 in the `usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64` build, 32 words, equal in objdump. Copies whose hash differs from the N32 routine are not listed here.

## Copies identical to a read copy

Each row is one routine in one build whose function-table code hash and size equal those of another copy of the same name that is already in this document, at its own address. The inventory computes each hash over that build's own bytes, so an equal hash and size mean equal bytes. The copy carries the reading of the copy it matches: the "Same bytes as" column names that copy's build and address, and the label is that copy's label. The rows were produced by a script that compared the function-table hashes, not by disassembling each copy in full. Two of them were checked by hand: `TargetIndexToTargetEnum` in `usr/gfx/arch/IP35ODSY/libGLcore.so` at 0xd95ac3c (80 bytes) and `__glOdyim_EvalCoord1dv` in `usr/gfx/arch/IP35ODSY64/libGLcore.so` at 0x21b9f60 (60 bytes) each have the same 32-bit instruction words, in objdump, as the matched copy. Routines whose copy differs from every read copy are not listed here.

## Copies read in full

Each entry is one routine in the o32 IP30 build (`usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so`, from `builds/6.5.30-media`), at that build's own address and size. The body was read in full, instruction by instruction, against the N32 routine named in [libglcore-odyssey-reads.md](libglcore-odyssey-reads.md), and each entry states how the two bodies differ. Offsets are the copy's own. **Established** means every difference the body has is stated; **Inferred** means part of the body was not compared.

### O32 IP30 routines read in full

- `sendBlenderOpTokens` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd950010, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as the N32 entry: the first, second and third arguments fill bits 15:13, 12:6 and 5:0 of the low 16 bits of the context word at +0x7c34, the dirty bit 0 of the word at +0x16960 is set, and process word 4120 is set to 2. The context is loaded from process word 4112. The copy has 32 instructions against N32's 26. It stores the +0x7c34 word three times (after the first field, after the second, and with the final value) where N32 stores once; the end state is the same. It also reads the word at context +11812 (0x2e24) and writes the same value back in the delay slot of the return, which N32 does not do. The field masks use the same constants as N32 (0xffff1fff, -8129 and -64).
- `DeallocCSetId` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9da074, 136 bytes):
  **Established.** Same algorithm as the N32 entry: the shift count is the word at +24 of the set, the node is reached by walking the first-level chain count times (count is the index shifted right by that amount), the table slot at the index masked to the node's range is zeroed, and the node's used count (+4) and the set's element count (+12) are each decremented. Differences: the mask is computed before the walk (N32 computes it after); the walk takes the count modulo 4 first, one node per step, and then four nodes per pass; the exit is a branch-likely (`beql`) that runs the final table load in its delay slot. N32 walks one node per pass and has no branch-likely.
- `__glOdysseyInitIgloo` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd971010, 144 bytes):
  **Established.** Sets the word at context +0x16980 to 1, bit 10 (0x400) of +0x16964 and bit 0 of +0x16988, stores 2 in process word 0x1018, then calls the entry at context +0x2e40 with the context. Differences: the callee gets the caller's a1 unchanged. In N32 a1 holds 0x188920 at the call, but only because the routine uses a1 as scratch for the gp offset (`lui` 0x19, `addiu` -30432); no code there passes it as an argument on purpose, so the value is incidental. It writes the word at +0x2e24 back to itself before the call. After storing process word 0x1018 it reloads that word and makes the call only when it reads 2 (a `bnel`), where N32 calls unconditionally. The frame saves gp and ra with `sw`.
- `__glOdyim_EvalPoint1` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd978240, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Same parameter as N32: the grid end at +0x790 when the index equals the division count at +0x798, otherwise the start at +0x78c plus index times ((end minus start) divided by the count). It then calls `__glOdysseyDoEvalCoord1` (0xd979994, reached through the slot at gp-31800) with the context. Differences: the difference and the division are computed on every path, where N32 computes them only when the index differs from the count; the parameter is returned as its bit pattern in a1 (`mfc1 a1,$f0`), where N32 passes it in f13; the frame saves ra and gp with `sw`; N32 also loads a slot at gp-31800 into t9 but calls the routine directly.
- `__glOdysseyDoEvalCoord1` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd979994, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** The same three calls as N32, in order: `GetCurrentState` (0xd979264) with dimension 1 and a record, the routine the table names `DoEvalCoord1` (0xd979624), and `RestoreCurrentState` (0xd979548) with the record. The three targets come from the slot at gp-32736 (value 0xd980000) minus 28060, 27100 and 27320. Differences: the copy keeps the context (a0) and the parameter (a1) in stack words 104 and 108 and reloads them for the second call, where N32 keeps the parameter in f30 across the first call and does not reload a1; the record is at sp+36 (N32 uses sp+0); the frame is 104 bytes. The parameter reaches the second call as the bit pattern in a1, which matches what `__glOdyim_EvalPoint1` passes in this build.
- `__glOdysseyInitFragmentLighting` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd98aa58, 136 bytes):
  **Inferred.** Writes the same five float words into the fragment light record as N32, at context +0x164b8, +0x164c8, +0x164d8, +0x164e4 and +0x164e8, and the same colour-material words at +0x165a8 (1032, 0x408) and +0x165ac (5634, 0x1602), and sets the word at +0x16970 to 1. Differences: the five floats (1.0, 1.0, -1.0, 180.0, 1.0, in the order of the five stores) are built from three immediates (0x3f800000, 0xbf800000, 0x43340000) inside a loop that runs once, where N32 loads three words from a constant table through a pc-relative address. The values in that N32 table were not compared with these immediates, so the **Inferred** label stands.
- `__glOdysseyAccumClose` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9501c8, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Same effect as N32: result 1; the image object numbers at +0x11ec0 and +0x11efc go back to -1, with their entry words at +0x11ec4 and +0x11f00 cleared, when they were set; the words at +0x12064 and +0x120ac go to -1 and the words at +0x12068 and +0x120b0 are cleared under the same tests; the byte at +0x120f5 is cleared on every path, as in N32. Differences: this build uses branch-likely (`beql`, `bnel`) for the skips and fills the delay slots with the next store or load; N32 uses plain `beq` with `nop` in the delay slot. Within the first block it writes -1 before zero, where N32 writes zero first. On one path it writes -1 to +0x12064 a second time, which is harmless.
- `__glOdyim_GetListParameterivSGIX` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9748e0, 104 bytes):
  **Established.** Converts the integer at the caller's pointer (the third argument, a2) to float, calls `__glOdyim_GetListParameterfvSGIX` (0xd9747f8, through the slot at gp-32052) with a pointer to a float temporary, and stores the truncated result back as an integer through the pointer. Differences: the temporary is at sp+36 (N32 uses sp+0 and stores the float in the call's delay slot); the copy saves the pointer at sp+48 and reloads it after the call, where N32 keeps it in s8; the integer is moved with `mfc1` and stored with `sw`, where N32 stores the float register with `swc1`; this build calls through the slot with `jalr`, and N32 loads a slot at gp-32052 into t9 and calls the routine directly without using it.
- `BindHyperpipe` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd96f3fc, 120 bytes):
  **Established.** Same effect as N32: a pipe number other than -1 calls `__glOdysseyBindHyperpipe` (0xda09438, through the slot at gp-29220) with the context and the number, and then stores the number at context +0x21ef0 (0x20000 + 7920); -1 calls `__glOdysseyUnbindHyperpipe` (0xda094ac, slot at gp-29216) with the stored number and stores nothing. Differences: the copy saves the context and the number in stack words 32 and 36 across the bind call and reloads them for the store, where N32 keeps them in s7 and s0; the test for -1 is a `bne` that falls into the bind path, where N32 branches to the unbind path with `beq`; the unbind path loads the stored number into a1 in the delay slot of the call and returns with a `b` that restores gp.
- `MakeCurrent` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd96f388, 116 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as the N32 entry: the context (a0) is saved at 40(sp), the host entry at context +60 is called with it and then the entry at +64, the first result is kept at 36(sp), and the routine calls `MakeCurrentRead` (0xd96e5b0, which the function table names for this build) with (context, first result, second result), returning that routine's v0. The last callee is reached through the slot at gp-32740 plus -6736, not a direct call. Differences: the N32 routine keeps the context in s0 and the first result in s8, and it ends with a branch-likely `bgezl` to 0xd96af00 (the delay slot pops the frame), so the N32 last call is a tail call; this copy makes an ordinary `jalr` and returns through its own epilogue (`lw ra,28(sp)`, `addiu sp,sp,40`). The frame is 40 bytes with ra at 28(sp) and gp at 24(sp), against N32's 32 bytes with ra at 0(sp).
- `__glOdyGetFromShadow` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd98bde8, 124 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as the N32 entry: the shadow pointer is read from context +93476 (0x16d24, `lui t7,1` and `lw t7,27940(t7)`) before the hardware read and saved at 36(sp). It calls `__glOdyGetFromHardware` (0xd98b3f8, GOT entry 273 through gp-31660) with the arguments unchanged and returns its result when that result is negative. Otherwise it calls `__glOdyDecodeShadowFormat` (0xd98b4e4, GOT entry 274 through gp-31656) with a0 = the shadow pointer plus 20 times n, where n is the caller's a2 reloaded from 48(sp), a1 = the caller's a3 from 52(sp), and a2 = the caller's fifth argument from 56(sp). It returns the decode routine's v0. Differences: the N32 routine also passes a3 = 20n and a4 = n to the decode routine, and this copy sets only a0, a1 and a2. The frame is 40 bytes with ra at 28(sp), gp at 24(sp), and the caller's a2 and a3 spilled to 48(sp) and 52(sp), where N32 uses a 48-byte frame with a2, a3 and a4 stored at 0, 8 and 16(sp).
- `__glOdyim_GetFogFuncSGIS` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd98d468, 124 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as the N32 entry: when the process word at 4120 is not 1, it copies 8 times the word at context +91184 (0x16430) bytes from context +91188 (0x16434) to the caller's pointer (a0, unchanged) with `memcpy` (GOT entry 3936 by the formula PLTGOT + 4*(local_gotno 3928 + dynsym 4285 - gotsym 4277)) and returns memcpy's v0. When that word is 1 it calls `__glSetError` (0xda4b3d8, GOT entry 1490) with 1282 and returns that result. The two words are read from process page 4096 with `lw t6,24(v1)` and `lw v0,16(v1)`, that is, from process addresses 4120 and 4112. Differences: the N32 routine tests the flag with `beq` and places its SetError call out of line at the end, while this copy tests with `bne` and makes the SetError call inline; the frame is 32 bytes with ra at 28(sp) and gp at 24(sp), against N32's 16 bytes with ra at 8(sp) and gp at 0(sp); the N32 routine reads the two words from absolute addresses with `lw ...,4112(zero)` and `lw ...,4120(zero)`.
- `QueryHyperpipeBestAttrib` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd96f474, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Same result as the N32 entry: the pipe number stored at context +138992 (0x21ef0) is negative, or the register a2 is not 1, and the routine returns 92; otherwise it calls `__glOdysseyQueryHyperpipeBestRect` (0xda08fd0, GOT entry 875 through gp-29252) with a0 = the context, a1 unchanged, a2 = the caller's a3, a3 = the caller's fifth argument and the fifth argument of the callee = the caller's sixth, stored at 16(sp), and returns that routine's v0. Differences: the N32 routine tests the stored number with `bltz` in v0, and the copy loads it into t6 and tests with `bgez`; the callee's arguments are the same values, but the copy passes the caller's fifth and sixth arguments from the stack (56(sp) and 60(sp)) where N32 passes them in a4 and a5; the `lw a3,56(sp)` in the delay slot of the `bne` on a2 runs on both paths, so a3 is overwritten even when the call is not made; the frame is 40 bytes with ra at 36(sp) and gp at 32(sp), against N32's 16 bytes; the result on the call path is moved through v1 before the return.

### 64-bit IP30 routines read in full

Each entry is one routine in the 64-bit IP30 build (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`), at that build's own address and size, read instruction by instruction against the N32 routine named in [libglcore-odyssey-reads.md](libglcore-odyssey-reads.md). Context and process-page addresses are the 64-bit build's own: a pointer is 8 bytes here, so the process word the N32 build reads as a 4-byte word at 4112 is read at 4128. The inventory credits each entry by its binary path and address.

- `AddKnownGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x220c2b0, 68 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 17 instructions in the same order as N32, with the 64-bit forms (`ld`, `sd`, `daddiu`) for the slot load, the frame and the saves. The call is to `AllocSpecificCSetId` at 0x220c680 (360 bytes in this build). The record's first word is loaded as a 32-bit word into a1 in the call's delay slot and loaded again as s8 after the call; v0 is 0 when the returned id equals that word and 1 otherwise, as in N32. The slot load into t9 and the `daddiu` on t9 are dead, as in N32.
- `DestroyKnownGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x220c3e0, 60 bytes):
  **Established.** Calls `RemoveKnownGroup` at 0x220c300 (212 bytes in this build) with a1 set to the element (the a0 of entry) and a0 loaded from process address 4128 (8 bytes) in the call's delay slot. It returns the callee's v0 unchanged, as N32 does. The frame is 16 bytes with ra at 0(sp) and gp at 8(sp). The slot load into t9 is dead.
- `__glOdyim_EvalCoord1dv` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21b9f60, 60 bytes):
  **Established.** Same steps as N32: the double at 0(a0) is loaded into f13 before the frame is set up, the context from process address 4128 goes in a0, and the call to `__glOdysseyDoEvalCoord1` (0x21bb5f0, 128 bytes) has the `the Odyssey stack assembly unit 1.d` narrowing in its delay slot. The slot load into t9 is dead. Differences: the 8-byte forms and the 4128 context address (N32 reads 4112 as a word).
- `__glOdyim_EvalCoord1fv` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21b9fe0, 60 bytes):
  **Established.** Same steps as N32: a0 is copied to at, the context from process address 4128 goes in a0, and the call to `__glOdysseyDoEvalCoord1` (0x21bb5f0) has the `lwc1` of the float at 0(at) into f13 in its delay slot. The slot load into t9 is dead. Differences: the 8-byte forms and the 4128 address, as in the double routine.
- `__glOdyim_EvalCoord2d` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21ba020, 64 bytes):
  **Established.** Same steps as N32: `mov.d f14,f13` and `the Odyssey stack assembly unit 1.d f13,f12` on the arguments, the context from process address 4128 in a0, and the call to `__glOdysseyDoEvalCoord2` (0x21c05c0, 140 bytes) with `the Odyssey stack assembly unit 1.d f14,f14` in its delay slot. Differences: the 8-byte forms and the 4128 address. The `mov.d` and `the Odyssey stack assembly unit 1.d` pairs are the same instructions as in N32.
- `__glOdyim_EvalCoord2f` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21ba0b0, 60 bytes):
  **Established.** Same steps as N32: `mov.s f14,f13` and `mov.s f13,f12` on the arguments, the context from process address 4128 loaded in the call's delay slot, and the call to `__glOdysseyDoEvalCoord2` (0x21c05c0). Differences: the 8-byte forms and the 4128 address. The slot load into t9 is dead.
- `__glOdyim_EvalCoord2fv` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21ba0f0, 64 bytes):
  **Established.** Same steps as N32: a0 is copied to at, the context from process address 4128 goes in a0, word 4(at) is loaded into f14 before the call, and word 0(at) into f13 in the call's delay slot.   **Established.** Same steps as N32. If the mode word at process address 4144 (N32 4120) is 1, it calls `__glSetError` (GOT local entry 1496) with 1282 and returns its result. Otherwise it stores the array argument a1 at 0(sp) in the delay slot of the target test, and compares the target a0 with 3553 (0xde1, GL_TEXTURE_2D). Any other target calls `__glSetError` with 1280 (0x500) and returns its result. For 0xde1 it calls `__glOdysseyGetCurrentTexture` (0x22140d0, GOT local entry 528) with the 8-byte context pointer at 4128 as a0 and the target as a1. Then it calls `memcpy` (global GOT slot 3873) with the saved array as the destination, the address held in the doubleword at +1296 of the returned texture object (0x510, N32 the word at +1288) as the source, and 8 times the word at +1292 (0x50c, N32 +1284) as the length: the count is doubled with `addu`, then shifted left by 2 in the delay slot. It returns memcpy's v0. Differences: the 8-byte pointers, the offsets above (the source is loaded as a doubleword here and as a word in N32), and the GOT slots for `memcpy` (3873 here, 3934 in N32) and `__glOdysseyGetCurrentTexture` (528 here, 527 in N32).
- `__glSoftAllocAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x2197760, 72 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as N32 with this build's offsets: the routine receives the context in a0 and the record in a1, and it calls the function pointer stored at context +40456 (0x9e08, formed with `li v0,0x9e08` and `daddu v0,v0,a0`). The pointer is a doubleword loaded with `ld t9,0(v0)` and the call is `jalr t9`. a0 is set to the record (`move a0,a1`) before the call. The third argument a2 is the word at context +4060 (0xfdc), loaded before a0 is overwritten. The first argument a1 is the word at context +4056 (0xfd8), loaded from `at` (a copy of the context) in the delay slot of the call. Differences: the 8-byte pointer and the callee slot at +0x9e08 (N32 calls through the 4-byte word at +0x7af0); the two argument words at +0xfd8 and +0xfdc (N32 reads +0xe74 and +0xe78); the doubleword frame and gp forms; 18 instructions against N32's 16, the two extra being the `li` and `daddu` that form the pointer address.
- `LookupCoresidRangeWithLevel` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x220c250, 88 bytes):
  **Established.** Same algorithm and branch structure as N32, with 8-byte list slots. a0 is not read. a1 is the base of a table of 8-byte per-level slots, a2 is the level, and a3 is copied to a4 and not used. For each level k from a2 down to 0 the routine reads the list head at a1 + 8k + 16, walks the list through the doubleword next pointer at node +0, and tests the word at node +20 with `slt` against a2. The first node whose count is not below a2 is returned in v0 (`jr ra` at 0x220c2a0). A negative a2 (`bltz` at 0x220c250) and a scan that finds no node both reach the tail call: the t9 load at 0x220c290 is followed by `bgezl zero` to `LookupCoresidRange` (0x220c010, 564 bytes), with a3 = a2 in its delay slot. Differences: the slot stride is 8 bytes (`dsll` by 3), the head is at +16 (N32 +12), and the node count is at +20 (N32 +12). The loop-back at 0x220c28c is a plain `bne`, and its delay slot (the t9 load) runs on every pass. N32 uses `bnel` (branch-likely), and its delay slot, the next head load, runs only when the branch is taken. This copy loads the head at the top of the loop on every pass.
- `sendBlenderOpTokens` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x2194000, 112 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as N32. The first, second and third arguments fill bits 15:13, 12:6 and 5:0 of the low 16 bits of the word at context +40836 (0x9f84, `lui a3,1` gives context + 0x10000, then `lw a6,-24700(a3)`). The masks are the same as N32's (0xe000 cleared, 0x1fc0 cleared with -8129, -64 for the low six bits). The dirty bit 0 of the word at context +101972 (0x18e54, the `lui a4,2` and `daddu v1,v1,a4` base plus -29100) is set, and process word 4144 is set to 2 in the delay slot of `jr ra`. The context is a doubleword at process address 4128. Differences: the 8-byte forms and the process and context addresses above (N32 reads the context at 4112, the global at 4120, the blend word at +0x7c34 and the dirty word at +0x16960). The routine has 28 instructions against N32's 26; the extra ones are the doubleword context load and the second `lui`/`daddu` pair that forms the dirty-word base.
- `initBuffersForAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21946d0, 112 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 28 instructions in the same order as N32, with the same calls. It calls `initVisualNumComp` (0x2194510) by a direct `bgezall` whose delay slot loads the context as a doubleword from process address 4128. It then calls `__glOdysseyImageDescribeWindow` (0x21cd480) twice through the GOT slot at gp-30344 (local entry 301, `ld t9`): first with a0 = context + 82384 (0x141d0) and a1 = 1, then with a0 = context + 82448 (0x14210) and a1 = 0. Finally it stores the byte 1 at context + 0x144b9 (`lui a2,1`, `ori a2,a2,0x44b9`, `daddu`), with `sb a1,0(a2)` in the delay slot of `jr ra`. Differences: the doubleword forms, the context address at process address 4128 (N32 4112), the descriptor offsets 0x141d0, 0x14210 and the byte at 0x144b9 (N32 0x11e38, 0x11e74 and 0x120f5), and the callee's own address 0x21cd480 (N32 0xd989710).
- `sendBlenderColorTokensAccum24` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x2194070, 124 bytes):
  **Established.** Same job as N32: the low 12 bits of the argument are placed in bits 11:0 and in bits 23:12 of two context words, and the dirty bit and the global are set as in `sendBlenderOpTokens`. The context is loaded as a doubleword from process address 4128 and the two words are at context +40840 (0x9f88) and +40844 (0x9f8c). The words are built with the same masks as N32 (0x00fff000 for the shifted value, 0xff000fff to keep the top byte and bits 11:0, and -4096 to clear the low 12 bits). The store order is the same as N32: this copy stores +0x9f8c (the N32 +0x7c3c word) first and +0x9f88 (the N32 +0x7c38 word) second, as N32 stores +0x7c3c and then +0x7c38. The dirty word is at context +101972 and the global at process address 4144 (N32 4120). The routine has 31 instructions against N32's 29.
- `InitialDestroyContext` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21aa0f0, 120 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 30 instructions and the same branch structure as N32. It reads the sharing ring pointer at context +102808 (0x19198) and the kernel record at context +102864 (0x191d0). If either is nonzero it calls `__glOdysseyUnSharePM` (0x220ac30) through the GOT slot at gp-29232 with a0 = context. Then it calls `InitialFree` (0x21aa0e0) by a direct `bgezall` with a0 = context, and it calls `__glDestroyContext` (0x2279d00) through the GOT slot at gp-20808 with a0 = context, returning its result. Differences: the doubleword forms, the two checked offsets (N32 +0x16c70 and +0x16c94), the gp constant built from `lui` 0x1a and `addiu` -12856, and the callee addresses and slots above (N32: UnSharePM 0xd9c66b0 through gp-30996, InitialFree 0xd965d20, __glDestroyContext 0xda34a60 through gp-26804).

The second call is `DoEvalCoord1` (0x21bb230) with the context in a0 and the saved float restored into f13 in its delay slot. The third is `RestoreCurrentState` (0x21bb180) with the record in a1 (`daddiu a1,sp,0` in its delay slot), after `ldc1 f31` restores the saved value. Differences: the saved float is f31 with `sdc1 f31,72(sp)` (N32 saves f30); the doubleword frame and gp forms; the gp constant built from `lui` 0x19 and `addiu` -18232; and the t9 loads before each call, from gp-32720 here (local GOT entry 4, the slot N32 also loads, at its own gp offset), each followed by an add of the callee's offset in both builds; the callees are entered by `bgezall` at labels inside the same function and do not read t9, so the loads are dead in both.
- `__glOdyssey_ResolveContext` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x219edc0, 128 bytes):

Differences: the doubleword forms; the context at process address 4128 (N32 4112); the two record offsets (N32 +0x168c8 and +0x168cc); the release entry at +24 (N32 +12); and the gp constant built from `lui` 0x1b and `addiu` -32520.

### O32 IP30 routines read from the body alone

The entries below cover routines that have no N32 counterpart entry in [libglcore-odyssey-reads.md](libglcore-odyssey-reads.md). Each was read from its own body in the o32 IP30 build (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so`) with the routine's fact sheet beside it, and every offset is this build's own. The async-marker routines share one state block at context +0x19ea8 (106152): two rings of 1025 sixteen-byte records, the first ring's head word at state +16400 and tail word at +16404, the second ring starting at state +16408, then four per-kind counters at context +0x21ed8, +0x21edc, +0x21ee0 and +0x21ee4 and a pending-marker word at +0x21ee8.

The following accumulation routines were read from their complete o32 bodies in `libGLcore.so`; their addresses and sizes are specific to that binary.

- `sendBlenderColorTokensAccum24` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd950090, 140 bytes): **Established.** Places the argument's low 12 bits in both 12-bit lanes of context words +0x7c38 and +0x7c3c, preserving their upper bytes. It sets dirty bit 0 at +0x16960, stores 2 at process address 0x1018 and rewrites +0x2e24; it emits no FIFO word.
- `destroyScratchBufAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd95011c, 300 bytes): **Established.** Releases the two scratch objects recorded at context +0x12064 and +0x120ac through their object entries at +0x12068 and +0x120b0. A failed release raises 0x505; the routine then clears the object numbers, entry words and descriptor state at +0x12064 through +0x120b4.
- `initScratchBufAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd950248, 1388 bytes): **Established.** Reuses the two 24-bit scratch objects when the saved dimensions at +0x12090/+0x12094 match +0xe74/+0xe78, both object numbers are present, and byte +0x120f4 is clear. Otherwise it marks the descriptors, destroys old objects, creates two rectangle-sized image objects, records their numbers and dimensions, and clears the dirty byte. Failed creation raises 0x505 and returns 0; success returns 1.
- `initVisualNumComp` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9507b4, 196 bytes): **Established.** Maps the format word at +0x11e08 through a seven-entry table and stores the component count at +0x12018: formats 0, 1, 2 and 4 give 1, format 3 gives 2, and formats 5 and 6 give 4 or 3 according to the flag at +0xd64. Values above 6 leave the destination unchanged.
- `initAccumGfxImages` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd950878, 316 bytes): **Established.** Fills hardware image descriptors at +0x11eb0 and +0x11eec with the window size, format 0xc, object numbers -3 and -4, entry words 4 and 5, and the address and size-exponent pairs from +0x16d70/+0x16d74 and +0x16d84/+0x16d88.
- `initBuffersForAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9509b4, 144 bytes): **Established.** Calls `initVisualNumComp`, describes the draw and read windows into records +0x11e38 and +0x11e74 with selectors 1 and 0, and sets byte +0x120f5. It returns no value.
- `initGeomForAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd950a44, 364 bytes): **Established.** Converts the rectangle at +0x11e28 through +0x11e34 into two work records at +0x1201c and +0x1202c, adding 1.0 before converting the upper coordinates. It stores the derived width and height at +0x12048/+0x1204c and +0x12058/+0x1205c.
- `softMult` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9525b0, 1812 bytes): **Established.** Multiplies every component in the soft accumulation rectangle described by record +0x7ab8. One is a no-op, zero clears, and other products are clamped to [-1, 1]; the body only reads and writes host pixel memory.
- `softAdd` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd952cc4, 1904 bytes): **Established.** Adds the incoming float to every component in the soft accumulation rectangle, leaving zero unchanged and clamping sums at the sign-appropriate endpoint of [-1, 1]. It returns no value.
- `softClear` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd954bd4, 676 bytes): **Established.** Allocates the soft pixel array through the record's allocator when its data pointer is null, then writes the four clear floats throughout the rectangle using the record's pixel size and row stride. It returns no value.
- `__glOdySoftAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd954d7c, 252 bytes): **Established.** Gets the context from process address 0x1010, allocates record +0x7ab8 when its data pointer +0x7ac8 is null, and dispatches operations 0x100 through 0x104 to the accumulation, load, return, multiply and add helpers. It has no direct board access.
- `__glOdyHardAccum24` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd957ca8, 568 bytes): **Established.** Initializes hardware buffers and scratch images, sets dirty bit 21 and process word 0x1018 to 2, then dispatches the five operations to the 24-bit helpers. Nonzero multiply/add magnitudes below 1/4096 become signed 1/4096, and non-return operations temporarily replace +0x1cc0 with +0x1d94.
- `__glOdyim_Accum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd957fec, 416 bytes): **Established.** Rejects calls inside begin/end, without an accumulation buffer, in colour-index mode, or with the required visual byte clear; unsupported operations raise 0x500. In render mode it saves and loads the library raster state, initializes buffers and geometry, calls the context operation at +0x1e24, restores raster state and returns.

- `__glOdysseyInitAccum` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd957ee0, 268 bytes): **Established.** Calls the context allocator/initializer with the two incoming records, then selects the accumulation format from the word at the second record +0xc9c. For format 16 it stores the first record's format word at context +0x18, installs the callback pointer at +0x6c and the value at +0x84, and returns 1. For format 24 it stores the corresponding second set, sets the auxiliary record's word +0x2064 to -1 and clears +0x2068, +0x206c, +0x20b0 and +0x20b4, then returns 1. Other formats return 0 without those stores. The complete branch and both record layouts are at 0xd957ee0.
- `__glOdysseyInitCI` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd966a8c, 292 bytes): **Established.** Calls the record initializer, then fills a colour-index buffer record from fixed tables: callback/format pointers at +0xa0, +0xa4, +0xa8, +0xac, +0xb8 and +0xbc, a table value at +0xb0, and another at +0xb4 and +0xc8. It derives the mask at +0x98 from the visual depth at the second argument +0xcb4, using `(1 << depth) - 1` for depths below 32 and -1 otherwise; it copies that mask to +0x48 and writes 1.0 to the four float pairs at +0x58/+0x5c, +0x88/+0x8c, +0x90/+0x94 and +0x80. The depth selects mode 4 below 17, mode 1 below 9, and mode 2 otherwise at +0x18. The full body is at 0xd966a8c.
- `__glOdysseyInitRGB` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9dce18, 240 bytes): **Established.** Calls the record initializer and fills an RGB buffer record from its fixed tables, writing pointers at +0xa0, +0xa4, +0xa8, +0xac, +0xb0, +0xb4, +0xb8, +0xbc and +0xc8. It sets +0x18, +0x70, +0x74, +0x78 and +0x7c to zero; +0x48, +0x4c, +0x50, +0x64, +0x68, +0x6c and +0x84 to one; and the float words at +0x58, +0x5c, +0x60, +0x80, +0x88, +0x8c, +0x90 and +0x94 to 1.0. The routine runs from 0xd9dce18 to its return at 0xd9dcf00.
- `__glOdysseyPickTextureProcs` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d75e8, 204 bytes): **Established.** Selects the context callback at +0x2f14. When the texture-enable word at +0x700 has bits 12, 11, 10 and the required mode bits clear, and the two texture mode words at +0x7c0 and +0x83c agree, modes 0x2400, 0x2401 and 0x2402 select three distinct callbacks. The same clean enable test without that mode agreement selects a fourth callback; any failed test selects the fallback callback. No board word or return register is changed. The complete decision tree is at 0xd9d75e8.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipPickPointProcs` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d7db8, 24 bytes): **Established.** Loads one callback pointer and stores it at context +0x3108. It has no condition, call or meaningful return value; the only state change is that one procedure-table slot at 0xd9d7db8.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipPickTriangleProcs` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d7dd0, 24 bytes): **Established.** Loads one callback pointer and stores it at context +0x2f60. It has no condition or call and returns through the link register; the complete three-instruction body is at 0xd9d7dd0.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipPickTriangleProcsFast` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d7de8, 24 bytes): **Established.** Same one-slot selector shape as the preceding routine, but loads a different callback pointer and stores it at context +0x2f60. It makes no call and returns immediately; the exact body is at 0xd9d7de8.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipPickTextureProcs` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d7e00, 244 bytes): **Established.** Chooses one of four texture callbacks at context +0x2f14. It combines the visual byte at +0xc94, enable bits 16, 17 and 18 of +0x700, the texture mode words at +0x7c0 and +0x83c, and the two state bytes at +0x584 and +0x585. It also tests bit 12 of +0x700 and the flag computed from those bytes, setting the local selection bits before the final choice. The routine writes no FIFO word and returns after the selected callback pointer is stored; every branch is in the body at 0xd9d7e00.
- `__glOdysseyInitLUTs` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d4930, 312 bytes): **Established.** Uses the context at process address 0x1010, initializes eight 0x80c-byte LUT records beginning at context +0x8000, and sets each record's kind/width fields to 6, 1 and -1 before calling the allocator callback with a 0x100-element, 0x10-byte, 8-bit description. It stops on the first callback result whose low byte is zero; only after all eight succeed does it set byte context +0xa741 to 1. The full loop and failure path are at 0xd9d4930.
- `__glInitDepth24` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda4e9e4, 228 bytes): **Established.** Calls the record initializer, then sets the depth-record format to 4, element size to 0x18 and its fixed callback/table pointers at +0x2c, +0x30, +0x38, +0x6c, +0x70, +0x78 and +0x7c. It writes the masks 0xff000000, 0x7fffff80 and 0x00ffffff at +0x50, +0x4c and +0x5c, sets +0x48 to -1, +0x58 to 7, clears +0x60 and writes the remaining zero/default words at +0x64. All stores are in the body at 0xda4e9e4.
- `__glInitDepth32` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda4eac8, 224 bytes): **Established.** Calls the record initializer and creates the 32-bit depth record with format 4 and element size 0x20. It installs the callback/table pointers at +0x2c, +0x30, +0x38, +0x6c, +0x70, +0x74, +0x78, +0x7c and +0x80, writes 0x7fffff80 at +0x4c, clears +0x50, +0x58 and +0x64, writes -1 at +0x48 and +0x5c, and sets byte +0x60 to 1. The complete body is at 0xda4eac8.
- `__glOdysseyDlistInterpret` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9738d0, 492 bytes): **Established.** Interprets the count-prefixed display-list stream at a0 and returns a pointer just past the consumed words. A zero low command id calls the context dispatch slot at +0x1c with the next word; id 1 calls slot +0x2c. Other ordinary words are skipped by their encoded operand count. Words with bit 31 set dispatch by the low nibble (15 is widened to 16) and the five-bit type at bits 28:24: types 1, 3 and 5 call three separate handlers with the operand count, low-ten-bit id and operand pointer. The loop subtracts each command's consumed words and advances over its operands. The complete stream walk is at 0xd9738d0.
- `__glOdysseyDlistInterpretPreclip` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd973abc, 592 bytes): **Established.** Interprets the same count-prefixed stream while applying the preclip dispatch rules. Ordinary ids 0 and 1 call the current context slots at +0x1c and +0x2c, while encoded words dispatch types 1, 3 and 5 to the corresponding handlers. Type 4 first checks the dispatch table's entry at +0x1f0 and either calls its entry for a matching id or falls back to the ordinary encoded handler. When that table does not match, the routine copies a run of operands through the temporary area at context +0x10000 before continuing. It returns the pointer saved at entry past the stream. The full branches and operand movement are at 0xd973abc.
- `__glOdysseyDlistPush` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd975e98, 820 bytes): **Established.** Decides whether a display-list push can use the cached path or must call one of two fallback routines. It reads the current context at process address 0x1010, rejects the fast path when context word +0x16988 has bits other than the permitted mask, and selects a mode from the visual byte +0xc94 and state bytes +0x169fc/+0x169fd. When the cached four-float record at the object +0x30 matches context +0x95b8 through +0x95c4, it marks the cache clean; otherwise it clears the object flag. Depending on context bit 4 at +0x16988 and the object flag at +0x24, it may call the allocator/validation callback with the object at +0x169f8, set the pending flags at +0x169fd/+0x169ff, and then call the alternate fallback. If no fallback is taken it decrements the stream count, optionally calls the list copy helper, and returns the advanced pointer. Every branch and store is in the 0xd975e98 body.
- `__glOdyim_Enable` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd958d64, 4620 bytes): **Established.** Dispatches the GL capability enumerant in a0 through the o32 jump tables and updates the context state at a1. Colour material calls its validation helper; vertex preclip updates +0x19db4/+0x19dc0 and the dirty bit. An invalid enumerant calls the error callback with 0x500, and an unavailable current context reports 0x502. The routine runs from 0xd958d64 to 0xd959f70; no path stores to the FIFO window.

Changed cases store 2 in process word 0x1018 and rewrite +0x2e24. Invalid enumerants call the error callback with 0x500, while an unavailable current context reports 0x502. The complete o32 body is at 0xd96022c and makes no FIFO store.

- `__glOdysseySoftClipPickClipProcsFast` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d7d80, 32 bytes):
  **Established.** Stores the address of `__glOdysseySoftClipClipLine` (0xda021b0, local GOT entry 757) at context +12540 and then the address of `__glOdysseySoftClipClipTriangleFast` (0xda02150, entry 756) at context +12144. The non-fast routine at 0xd9d7d60 differs only in the second address (entry 754, 0xda01e68).

- `__glOdyim_Fogf` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9846c4, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Dispatches a fog float through the table selected by `a0 - 0xb61` when that unsigned value is below 5, passing the value's stack copy at `sp+0x24`. An out-of-range selector calls the error path with 0x500. The table target and error path are reached through this build's GOT, and the routine returns after restoring its 0x20-byte frame.
- `__glOdyim_FragmentLightiSGIX` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd98636c, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Accepts fragment-light parameter selectors 0x1205 through 0x1209 (`a1 - 0x1205` below 5), saves the integer value from `a2` at `sp+0x28`, and dispatches through the selector table. Any other selector reports error 0x500. The function has no direct board store; its selected helper receives the saved value pointer.
- `__glOdyim_FragmentLightModelfSGIX` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9868e0, 116 bytes):
  **Established.** Accepts only selectors 0x8408, 0x8409 and 0x840b, saves the float argument at `sp+0x24`, and calls the selected fragment-light-model helper. Other selectors use the error path with 0x500. The accepted selector tests are explicit branches at 0xd9868fc, 0xd986908 and 0xd986910.
- `__glOdyim_FragmentMaterialiSGIX` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd98a8e4, 96 bytes):
  **Established.** Handles only parameter 0x1601 in `a1`; for that parameter it saves `a2` at `sp+0x28` and calls the fragment-material helper with its address. All other parameters report error 0x500. No board address is written directly by this wrapper.
- `__glOdyim_lmcolor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd98e228, 76 bytes):
  **Established.** Reads the board record pointer from process word 0x1010 and changes bit 0 of its word at +0x7b40: a low-byte argument of 1 sets the bit, and every other value clears it. It then stores 2 to process word 0x1018, sets bit 0 of the board-record word at +0x2e24, and returns without a call. The final board-record update is in the return delay slot at 0xd98e270.
- `__glOdyim_ConvolutionParameterfEXT` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9926bc, 100 bytes):
  **Established.** Accepts only parameter 0x8013 in `a1`, saves the float bits from `a2` at `sp+0x24`, and passes that pointer to the convolution-parameter helper. Other parameters call the error path with 0x500. The helper call and the error call are mutually exclusive and the 0x28-byte frame is restored on both paths.
- `__glOdyim_ConvolutionParameteriEXT` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd992948, 96 bytes):
  **Established.** Uses the same single accepted selector, 0x8013, but saves the integer from `a2` at `sp+0x24` before calling the integer convolution helper. A different selector reports 0x500. The routine returns no helper result and restores its 0x28-byte frame.
- `__glOdyim_PixelTexGenParameteriSGIS` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd993e28, 108 bytes):
  **Established.** Accepts pixel-texgen parameters 0x8354 and 0x8355, saves the integer value from `a1` at `sp+0x24`, and passes its address to the parameter helper. Any other parameter reports error 0x500. The two accepted values share one helper path; the invalid path makes no state update.
- `__glOdyim_GetPixelTexGenParameterivSGIS` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd993f94, 172 bytes):
  **Established.** Rejects the query with error 0x502 when process word 0x1018 equals 1. Otherwise selectors 0x8354 and 0x8355 load words from the context at +0x11e10 and +0x11e14 respectively and store the result through `a1`; other selectors call the error path with 0x500. The context pointer is read from process word 0x1010.
- `__glOdyim_PixelTexGenParameterfSGIS` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd994040, 108 bytes):
  **Established.** Accepts only 0x8354 and 0x8355 in `a0`, saves the float value from `a1` at `sp+0x24`, and calls the selected pixel-texgen helper with that pointer. Other selectors report 0x500. This wrapper performs no direct FIFO or board-record store.
- `__glOdyim_Lightf` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d2884, 156 bytes):
  **Established.** Rejects the operation with error 0x502 when process word 0x1018 equals 1. Otherwise it converts the float bits in `a2` through `f12`, calls the light-value helper with the original selector and converted value, then calls a second helper with the saved process pointer, the original light selector and the value. The second helper's address is formed from the GOT word at gp-0x7fcc plus 0x2740.
- `__glOdyim_Lighti` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d29b8, 152 bytes):
  **Established.** Has the same process-state guard and two-helper sequence as the float form, but passes the integer value in `a2` unchanged to the first helper. The second helper receives the process pointer, light selector and integer value; its target is the same GOT-derived address at 0xd9d2a2c. Error 0x502 is used while process word 0x1018 is 1.
- `__glOdyim_LightModelf` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d2b20, 160 bytes):
  **Established.** Rejects while process word 0x1018 is 1 with error 0x502. Otherwise it converts `a1` to a float argument for the model-value helper, then ORs 0x140 into the context word at +0x16964, stores 2 to process word 0x1018, and rewrites the context word at +0x2e24 with itself to mark the update. The context is read from process word 0x1010.
- `__glOdyim_LightModeli` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9d2c90, 156 bytes):
  **Established.** Mirrors the float model wrapper but passes the integer `a1` unchanged to its helper. After a non-error call it sets bits 8 and 6 of the context word at +0x16964 through OR mask 0x140, stores 2 to process word 0x1018 and rewrites +0x2e24. Process word 0x1018 equal to 1 selects error 0x502 instead.
- `__glOdyim_Scissor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xd9f7264, 128 bytes):
  **Established.** Reads the context pointer from process word 0x1010 and guards on process word 0x1018. When the latter equals 1 it reports error 0x502; otherwise it calls the context's helper at +0x2ebc with the context pointer. Before that call it invokes a second helper through the GOT with no visible arguments, and restores the 0x28-byte frame before returning.

### O32 vpro-patch-4103 routines read in full

Each entry is one routine in the o32 IP30 library of the 4103 patch (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so`), read in full from its own disassembly. Sizes are the routine's own extent (the function table's size for several of these runs on into the static routines behind them). Offsets are this build's own: the context record is laid out differently from the 6.5.30 builds, and every FIFO store goes to the absolute window address 0x4000. A "settle prefix" below is the four-word sequence `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, sent first when the settle-needed word at context +0x9750 is set (which clears it).

- `DrawBitmapUsingPolyStipple` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda3f914, 3100 bytes):
  **Established.** Draws one bitmap through `LoadAndSendStippledQuads` (0xda3ef20, reached through the local slot at gp-32740 plus -4320) after switching the board to polygon-stipple mode, then puts the state back (arguments: a0 context, a1 bitmap record, a2 pointer to the bitmap bytes; the last two are saved at sp+516 and sp+520). It returns at once, with no value set, when the byte at context +0x16658 is nonzero. Otherwise: (1) it compares the pair at +0x7b40 and +0x7b44 with the pair at +0x16650 and +0x16654; when either differs it copies both across, sends block `0x1081d` with the two words (after a settle prefix when needed), and, when bit 20 of the word at +0x7a90 is clear, copies a four-word template from the data slot at gp-32616 plus 4144 to the stack, rewrites its four words with the 12-bit fields of the two pairs (low 12 bits and bits 23:12 of each word) and calls `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0, slot gp-30572) with request 0x6f64000d. It then sets bit 13 (0x2000) of the dirty word at +0x168d4, stores 2 in process word 0x1018 and rewrites the word at +0x2d44 with itself. When the pairs match this step is skipped, branch-likely. (2) It saves the word at +0x7b18 (sp+500), the 32 stipple words at +0x7a98 to +0x7b14 (16 words at +0x7a98 to +0x7ad4 to sp+428 to sp+488, the other 16 at +0x7ad8 to +0x7b14 to sp+364 to sp+424, one pair per pass), +0x7b1c (sp+492) and the word at +0x9748 (sp+360). When the byte at +0xa65c is set it calls `__glOdysseyUpdateRasterPos` (0xda68694, slot gp-31672) with the context, and it always calls `__glOdysseyImageCalcOdyEyeSpaceRasterPosZ` (0xdaa6688, slot gp-31188) and keeps the float it returns at sp+356. (3) When the pointer at +0x164c0 (the bound texture) is nonzero it takes the current texture coordinates at +0x120, +0x124 and +0x128 and divides all three by the word at +0x12c (computed as 1.0 divided by it in double precision) unless that word is zero. When the third is nonzero (compared as a double) and the word at +12 of the bound texture is 0x806f, it goes through the dispatch table at +0x17ec: it saves the error code returned by slot +492 (sp+112), the matrix mode (slot +500 with 0xba0, sp+108), pushes the projection, model-view and texture matrices (modes 0x1701, 0x1700, 0x1702 through slot +620, push through slot +640), and each time slot +492 returns 0x503 it instead reads the matrix with slot +496 (enumerants 0xba7, 0xba6 and 0xba8 into the buffers at sp+276, sp+212 and sp+148; a byte flag at sp+343, 342 or 341 records which). It loads identity (slot +608) in each, makes the projection an orthographic box through slot +632 (left 0.0, right the integer at +0xe6c, bottom 0.0, top the integer at +0xe70, near -1.0, far 1.0), applies slot +664 with 0.0, 0.0 and the word at +0x108, and saves the current colour (enumerant 0xb00 into the buffer at sp+132) and texture coordinate (0xb03 into the buffer at sp+116) with slot +496 before setting them from the words at +0xf4 (slot +1428, with that word as the argument) and the address +0x120 (slot +1660). Which GL call each slot is was inferred from its arguments. (4) It clears bits 4, 3 and 2 of +0x7b18 and sets bit 2, then clears bits 1:0, so bits 4:0 hold 4 (three stores of the word); it clears bit 11 of +0x7b1c when bit 6 of the word at +0x700 is set and bit 7 when bit 24 of +0x700 is set; it sets bit 4 of +0x7b1c and sends block `0x10404` with it. This build sends no `0x1043f` here; the callee sends it. When the polygon-mode words at +0x1e4 or +0x1e8 differ from GL_FILL (0x1b02) it saves +0x7b24 (sp+496), sets its bits 3:2 to 10 and bits 1:0 to 10, and sends `0x10415` with it. It clears the low 12 bits of the word at +0x9748 and sends block `0x10456` with the word. This build has no clip-plane block `0x10405`. Each send has its own settle prefix. (5) When the word at +0x168d0 is nonzero it calls `__glOdysseyValidateHardware` (0xda47694, slot gp-32192). It then calls `LoadAndSendStippledQuads` with the context, the bitmap record, the float from sp+356, the float from sp+352, the floats from sp+348 and sp+344 and the bytes pointer on the stack. (6) It restores: +0x7b18 from sp+500, the 32 stipple words (four words of each half per pass), and resends `0x1043f` with +0x7b18, `0x13c4e` with the 16 words at +0x7a98 to +0x7ad4, `0x13c4f` with the 16 words at +0x7ad8 to +0x7b14, `0x10404` with +0x7b1c restored from sp+492, `0x10415` with +0x7b24 restored from sp+496 when a polygon-mode word is not GL_FILL, and `0x10456` with +0x9748 restored from sp+360. When the bound texture is 3D and the third coordinate was nonzero it pops the three matrix modes (slot +636, or slot +612 on the saved matrix when the push had overflowed), restores the colour and texture coordinate, the matrix mode from sp+108, and finally calls `__glSetError` (0xdb1ac34, slot gp-26932) with the error code saved at sp+112. It returns with no value set on every path. Compared with the 6.5.30 N32 routine of the same name it has the same stages, uses the context offsets given here, writes the FIFO at absolute 0x4000, and sends no clip-plane block `0x10405`.
- `OpenPipeMemReference` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaaccc4, 516 bytes):
  **Established.** Marks a range as in use and makes the board hold it (arguments: a0 context, a1 group record, a2 range record, a3 new extent, and the mode as the fifth argument at 88(sp)). When the mode is 0, the word at range +0x1c is 1 and the word at range +0x18 plus the extent is above zero, it first drains the pipe by sending 17 constant words to the FIFO at 0x4000: `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, `0x10019`, `0x10443`, 150, four `0x10046`, then, after the eleventh word, a zero stored to the settle word at +0x9750, then `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, `0x10658` and `0x120000`. It then stores the extent at range +0x18 and the mode at range +0x1c. When the word at group +0x50 is zero it walks the first two words of the block that the pointer at context +0x16bac names, stops at the first one that holds -1 and stores the group's first word (its id) there, and stores nothing if neither holds -1. It sets the halfword at +6 of the counter record that range +0x24 points to (the 16-byte counter record) to 1 and adds 1 to its halfword at +4, then calls `__glOdysseyMIPS_SYNC` (0xdba4b90, local GOT entry 3005, a `jr ra` whose delay slot is `sync`). The ioctl is skipped, returning 1, only when the counter record's halfword at +2 is zero and its halfword at +0 is nonzero (the two `bnezl` at 0xdaace34 and 0xdaace40); otherwise it builds a request at sp+48 (the group's first word, then the halfwords from range +8 and range +0xc) and sends ioctl `0x6f640003` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0). A zero result returns 1. A nonzero result takes 1 off the +4 halfword, zeroes the +6 halfword, calls `__glOdysseyMIPS_SYNC` again and returns 0. Differences from the 6.5.30 N32 routine: the same drain words and ioctl, with this build's offsets (the block pointer at +0x16bac where N32 uses +0x16c9c, the settle word at +0x9750 where N32 uses +0x9830), and a `sync` after each counter change, which N32 does not do.
- `__glOdysseyGetHardwareShadow` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdabf724, 96 bytes):
  **Established.** Sends private ioctl `0x6f64000c` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0, slot gp-30572) with the context (a0, kept at 32(sp)) and the second argument moved into a2 as the data pointer. A result of zero or more is returned unchanged. A negative result makes it call the function at offset 20 of the context, with the context and the string at 0xdbbb940 ("the context-manager save hardware shadow Odyssey driver request: failed", read from the library's .rodata, local GOT entry at gp-32644 plus -18112), and return what that call returns. The shape matches the 6.5.30 o32 routine, with this build's own gp constants and string address.
- `__glOdysseyAssignToPMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaaca14, 368 bytes):
  **Established.** Sends host data to a pipe-memory object (arguments: a0 context, a1 group id, a2 entry index, a3 host address, and on the stack the length, the flags, a caller word and a pointer to the eight-word transfer header at 148(sp)). It first masks the header's word at +20 with 0x1ffff in place, then fills an 80-byte request at sp+40 that `bzero` (slot gp-17196) cleared: 1 is kept in the separate word at sp+36, the group id at request +4, the entry index (a2, stored with `sh` at sp+48), the host address at +0x14 with its sign extension in the word at +0x10, the length at +0x18, the eight header words at +0x1c to +0x38, the flags with bit 0 forced on at +0x3c and the caller word at +0x40. It calls `CheckMMXfrArgs` (0xdaac9ac) with the request and returns 0 when that returns 0. It sends ioctl `0x6f640006` (slot gp-30572); a nonzero result puts 0 in sp+36 and returns it. On success it stores zero at context +0x21de0 when bit 1 of the request's flags word is set (the caller asked for a wait), and otherwise the request word at +0x4c, which the kernel rewrote; it returns 1. This build's context offset is 0x21de0 where the 6.5.30 N32 routine uses +0x21ee8.
- `__glOdysseyReadFromPMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaacb84, 320 bytes):
  **Established.** The read direction of the same transfer, with the same arguments and request layout (header word +20 masked with 0x1ffff in place, the sign-extended host address at request +0x10 and +0x14, the same `CheckMMXfrArgs` check, ioctl `0x6f640006`, the same result handling, zero or the request word at +0x4c stored at context +0x21de0 and 1 returned). The only difference from `__glOdysseyAssignToPMObj` is that the flags are stored exactly as passed (the sixth argument, passed on the stack, goes to request +0x3c unchanged, with no `ori 1`). It keeps only a0 in a stack slot (120(sp)) because it never reads a1 to a3 again after storing them.
- `__glOdysseyCreatePMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaac57c, 464 bytes):
  **Established.** Asks the kernel for a new memory object (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to a ten-word description, a2 pointer to the object number, which is read and rewritten, a3 a halfword). The request at sp+56 holds -1 at +0, the number from a2 at +4, a3 as a halfword at +8, the ten words of the description at +0xc to +0x33 (copied three words per pass for three passes and then one word) and 0 at +0x34. It sends ioctl `0x6f640001` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0). When the ioctl returns zero and the number the kernel left at request +4 is -1 it returns 0 and stores nothing. When it returns zero and that number is not -1 it stores the number through a2, and, when bit 1 of the request word at +0x34 is set, also stores the request word at +0x3c into the table at +0x1c of the kernel record named by the word at context +0x16ba4, at the index given by the halfword at request +0x40; it returns 1 either way. When the ioctl returns nonzero it returns 0: unless the request number is -1 and the caller's own number (the word a2 points at) is not -1, it does nothing more; in that one case it first walks every context in the sharing set and removes that number's group record from each (`InitCSetCursor` at 0xdaae0b4, then `NextCSetElem` at 0xdaae0c8 on the word at +0x16ba4, `LookupKnownGroup` with creation off, `RemoveKnownGroup` at 0xdaadbb0), then stores the -1 through a2. The store to a2 at 0xdaac700 is unreachable: it follows the unconditional branch at 0xdaac6f8 and nothing branches to it; the branch-likely `bnel` at 0xdaac6f0 stores the number in its own delay slot.
- `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaac868, 324 bytes):
  **Established.** Frees a memory object (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to the object number, a2 a count). It returns 0 at once when a2 is negative or 17 or more. It fills a request at sp+48 with the number at +4, a2 as a halfword at +8 and 1 at +0x34 (the +0 word is not set), and sends ioctl `0x6f640002` through `__glOdysseyIoctl`; a nonzero result returns 0. When the request's word at +0x34 is nonzero afterwards (the kernel keeps the object) it returns 1. Otherwise it walks every context in the sharing set (`InitCSetCursor` then `NextCSetElem` on the word at context +0x16ba4), calling `LookupKnownGroup` with creation off and the number and, where one is found, `RemoveKnownGroup`; it then stores -1 through a1 and returns 1.
- `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObjGroup` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaac74c, 284 bytes):
  **Established.** Frees a whole memory object, in the opposite order to `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObj` (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to the object number). It first walks every context in the sharing set and removes the number's group record from each (the same `InitCSetCursor`, `NextCSetElem`, `LookupKnownGroup` with creation off, `RemoveKnownGroup` loop on the word at +0x16ba4), then sends ioctl `0x6f640002` with the number at request +4 and 0 at +0x34 (the +0 and +8 words are not set). A nonzero result returns 0; a zero result stores -1 through a1 and returns 1.
- `LookupKnownGroup` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaad6e4, 356 bytes):
  **Established.** Finds the group record for an object number (arguments: a0 context, a1 number, a2 create flag at 64(sp)). The numbers -3 and -4 return 0. Otherwise `LookupCSetId` (0xdaae27c) is called on the set at context +0x16b88 and a nonzero answer is returned. With no record stored it returns 0 unless the create flag is set; with the flag it calls the context's allocator (the function at offset 0 of the context) for 84 bytes. A zero from the allocator raises `__glSetError` 1285 (0x505) and returns 0. Otherwise it zeroes the 84 bytes with `bzero`, stores the number in the first word, calls `AddKnownGroup` (0xdaadb54) on the set at +0x16b88 and ignores its result, and calls `LookupCoresidRange` (0xdaad848) with level 0, count 15 and create on. When that returns a record it returns the new group record; otherwise it calls `RemoveKnownGroup` (0xdaadbb0) on the group and returns 0. The first `beq` at 0xdaad708 skips the -4 test when the number is -3 and branches to the return of 0.
- `LookupCoresidRange` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaad848, 624 bytes):
  **Established.** Finds, or asks the kernel to describe, a range record of a group (arguments: a0 context, a1 group, a2 level, a3 count, the create flag at 112(sp)). It scans the list whose head is the word at group +0xc plus four times the level and returns the first record whose count at +0xc is not below a3 (the scan uses branch-likely `beqzl` and `bnezl`). With no match and the create flag zero it returns 0. With the flag it takes 44 bytes from the context's allocator (a zero raises `__glSetError` 1285 and returns 0), zeroes them and presets the record: group at +4, level at +8, count at +0xc, zeros at +0, +0x10 and +0x14, -1 at +0x18 and +0x1c. It sends ioctl `0x6f640004` with a request at sp+48: the word 2, the group's first word at sp+52, and the level and count as halfwords at sp+56 and sp+58. On a nonzero result it releases the record through the context's release entry (the function at offset 12) and returns 0. On a zero result the kernel has overwritten the request buffer, and the routine reads these reply fields (the routine does not store them; byte numbers count from the buffer start at sp+48): when bit 4 of the reply's first word is set it stores the reply word at byte 28 of the request buffer into the word at context +0x16bac plus four times the reply halfword at bytes 32 and 33; it stores the reply halfword at bytes 12 and 13 (the object handle) in group +4, sets group +8 to the table word at [kernel record at +0x16ba4] +0x1c plus four times the handle's high byte, plus 0x40 times its low byte, and stores the reply halfword at bytes 14 and 15 in group +0x50; it stores the reply halfword at bytes 16 and 17 in record +0x20 and points record +0x28 at the status halfword at [+0x16ba8] plus twice that index; it stores the reply halfword at bytes 18 and 19 in record +0x22 and points record +0x24 at the 16-byte counter record that the index's low ten bits select in the block named by its high six bits (blocks from +0x16bac); it copies the status halfword into the counter record's +4, zeroes its +6 and +8, links the record at the head of the level's list and returns it. This is the N32 routine's algorithm with this build's offsets (+0x16ba4, +0x16ba8 and +0x16bac where N32 uses +0x16c94, +0x16c98 and +0x16c9c).
- `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda44c9c, 412 bytes):
  **Established.** Refreshes the context's window origin and size (argument: a0 context; no value is set on return). It seeds the width and height locals with the words at +0xe6c and +0xe70, then calls the callback at context +0x1c with the context, two origin locals, the width local and (fifth argument, on the stack) the height local. Unlike the 6.5.30 N32 routine it has no kernel query: there is no ioctl and no byte test before the callback. It stores the float of the returned origin x plus the float at +0x19cb4 at +0x19cac, and the float of origin y plus that same float at +0x19cb4 at +0x19cb0 (both sums add the one word at +0x19cb4). It compares the returned width and height with +0xe6c and +0xe70 and calls `__glChangeWindowSize` (0xdb1aeb8, local GOT entry 1457) with the context, width and height when either differs (the height compare is branch-likely). It then calls the callback at +0x48 with the context for the read-window width and height, compares them with +0xe74 and +0xe78, and calls `__glChangeReadWindowSize` (0xdb1afd0, entry 1458) with them when either differs. When the word at +0x168f0 is nonzero it calls `__glChangeWindowSize` again with the words at +0x6a0 and +0x6a4. Finally, when the byte at +0xc90 is nonzero and the draw width or height from the first callback still differs from +0xe6c or +0xe70 as they stand after those calls, it sets the byte at +0x11fc0 to 1. The 6.5.30 N32 routine makes the read-window callback conditional on a byte (+0xdd0 there) and kicks off with the kernel query described in its entry; this build does neither.

A negative result calls the function at context +0x14 with the context and the string "odsy: MakeCurrent ioctl failed" (0xdbbb990, local GOT entry at gp-32644 plus -18032) and returns 0. On success the kernel has overwritten the buffer, and the routine copies from it (byte numbers count from the buffer start at sp+44): the word at byte 4 to +0x16c50, the word at byte 8 to +0x16c54, byte 34 to +0x16c58, the halfword at byte 32 to +0x16c5c and byte 35 to +0x16c60. With 1 it stores the word at byte 12 to +0x16c78, the word at byte 8 to +0x16c7c, byte 37 to +0x16c80, the halfword at byte 32 to +0x16c84, the word at byte 16 to +0x16c8c, then byte 8 to +0x16c90, byte 37 to +0x16c94 and the halfword at byte 32 to +0x16c98, and zeroes 20 bytes at +0x16ca0 and at +0x16cb4. - `ApplyParameterF` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda3bacc, 1188 bytes):
  **Established.** Sends one material parameter to the board (arguments: a0 the side, kept in s1, nonzero for front; a1 the parameter enumerant; a2 a pointer to four floats, kept in s0; the context is the process word at 0x1010, kept at sp+68; the mdebug size of 1784 in the function table runs on into the next two routines, this one ends at 0xda3bf70). It starts with a3 = 0, a mask of the colour blocks to send, and tests a1 in this order: 0x1200 gives mask 1, 0x1201 mask 2, 0x1202 mask 4, 0x1600 mask 8, 0x1601 the shininess branch, 0x1602 mask 3 and 0x1603 the colour-index branch; any other value keeps mask 0 and the routine returns with v0 = 0, raising no error. The byte at context +0xc8c (a `lbu` at each case and at 0xda3bb50) gates the mask: when it is zero, a colour-index visual, the mask is cleared at 0xda3bec8 and nothing is sent. Otherwise the loop at 0xda3bed4 walks the mask from bit 0 up. For each set bit v it writes to the absolute window 0x4000 the word `0xc0800000 | 0xd000 | ((v + 6) << 24) | (v + 47)` for the front side, or `0xc0800000 | 0xd000 | ((v + 10) << 24) | (v + 53)` for the back, then the four floats of a2 unchanged. Front gives 0xc680d02f, 0xc780d030, 0xc880d031 and 0xc980d032 (ambient, diffuse, specular, emission) and back 0xca80d035 to 0xcd80d038. Ambient-and-diffuse (mask 3) sends the first two blocks. On leaving the loop v0 is the last bit number plus one, which no caller reads.
  Shininess (0x1601): a2[0] is widened to a double and compared with 2.5 (the pair built at 0xda3bbcc) and with 0.0. A value above 0 and below 2.5 is replaced by 2.5; zero, negative and 2.5 or more are used as they are (the `bc1fl` at 0xda3bc0c takes the square root in its delay slot on the not-above-zero path, and the fall-through reaches the same `sqrt.d` at 0xda3bc20). The root is rounded to a float and compared with the cached float at context +0x9734 (front) or +0x9738 (back): the index is 0 for front and 1 for back, times 4, plus 0x8000 plus 5940. When they are equal nothing is sent and the routine returns. Otherwise it stores the root in the cache and sends `0xd080c433` (front) or `0xd080c439` (back), the root, `0x908091ea`, the front cache float and its negation, then the back cache float (+0x9738) and its negation, and calls `__glOdysseySetProgram` (0xdab0010, local GOT entry 441; the `jalr` is at 0xda3bcdc) with a0 the context, a1 the word at +0xa5c8, a2 0x8000 and a3 0. It then rejoins the common tail and returns with v0 = 0.
  Colour indexes (0x1603): when the byte at +0xc8c is nonzero (RGB visual) nothing is sent. When it is zero the routine builds four floats at sp+80 to sp+92. If a2[2] is 0.0 all four are 0.0. Otherwise sp+80 is a2[0], sp+84 is (a2[1] minus a2[0]) plus 0.5, sp+88 is (a2[2] minus a2[0]) plus 0.5 (both sums in double, rounded to float) and sp+92 is 0.0. Two passes then run (v0 = 0 and 2; the source pointer steps by 8). Each pass sends the word with (v0 + 6) and (v0 + 47) (back: v0 + 10 and v0 + 53), the float at the pointer and three 0.0 stores, then the word with v0 + 7 and v0 + 48 (back: v0 + 11 and v0 + 54), the float at the pointer plus 4 and three 0.0 stores. Per enumerant (a0 offsets in bytes): 0x1200, three floats: the first in the word at 0, the second in the halfword at 2, the third in the word at 4 (the fourth float is not read). 0x1201, four floats: word at 8, halfword at 10, word at 12, halfword at 14. 0x1202, three floats: word at 16, halfword at 18, word at 20. 0x1600, three floats: word at 24, halfword at 26, word at 28. 0x1602 (ambient and diffuse together), four floats read once and each stored twice: the first in the words at 0 and 8, the second in the halfwords at 10 and 2, the third in the words at 4 and 12, the fourth in the halfword at 14 only, so the ambient alpha slot (halfword 6) is not written. 0x1601 (shininess) is different: the float is widened to a double and, when it is above the double 127.968775 (the pool value 0x405ffe0068db8bac at 0xdbb9d18), the halfword at 30 gets its low twelve bits set (`ori 0xfff`); otherwise the value times 32.0 is converted with `cvt.w.d` (the same unsigned sequence, with 2^31 as a double) and its low twelve bits replace those of the halfword at 30.

## Copies credited by word comparison

Each item is one routine in one build, at that build's own address and size. Its instruction words were compared with every word of another copy of the same routine, which is read in full elsewhere in this document or in the kernel chapter. The comparison covers all words of the routine on the file bytes, as the 32-bit big-endian words that objdump prints. Equal words mean equal code, so the item takes the label of the copy it matches.

- `ComputeFirstPartials` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x320b670, 156 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 39 words as the N32 routine at 0xd977c70 in `builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, all compared word for word. The body is the N32 one: it converts the partial derivatives of a 4-component vertex map into derivatives of the divided position with `lwc1`, `mul.s`, `msub.s` and `swc1`. It has no call and no gp-relative load, so it touches no board register. Code hash 6652c635ac34.
- `ComputeFirstPartials` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP35ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21bb670, 156 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 39 words as the N32 routine at 0xd977c70 in `builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, all compared word for word, with the same body and no call or gp-relative load. Code hash 6652c635ac34.
- `ComputeFirstPartials` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x21bb670, 156 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 39 words as the N32 routine at 0xd977c70 in `builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, all compared word for word, with the same body and no call or gp-relative load. Code hash 6652c635ac34.
- `ComputeNormal2` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x320b710, 100 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 25 words as the 6.5.30 64-bit routine at 0x21bb710 in `builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, all compared word for word. Its vectors are A in a3 and B in a2. Word 9 (0x320b724) is `bc3fl` on FP condition code 2, branching out of the routine to 0x321dfc8. Its delay slot stores A1*B2 to a1+0, and that store runs only when the branch is taken: with the flag set, the store is skipped. The routine never sets condition code 2, so the branch depends on state the caller leaves. The stores to a1+4 and a1+8 are two-term differences, the cross-product terms for those components. The term A2*B1 that the a1+0 component needs is loaded at 0x320b710 and 0x320b71c and never used, so the +0 value is only the first product. The fall-through path tail-calls the context entry at +18488 (0x4838) with `move a0,a1` in the delay slot. Code hash 62b0f2f28ffe.
- `MakeCurrent` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, 0x32004a0, 108 bytes):
  **Established.** Same 27 words as the 64-bit routine at 0x21b04a0 in `builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY64/libGLcore.so`, all compared word for word. It saves the context in s0, calls the host entries at context +120 and +128 (`ld t9,120(a0)` and `ld t9,128(s0)`, each with `jalr t9`), and then loads the gp slot at -32728, subtracts 0xd50 and leaves the result in t9, then ends with the branch-likely `bgezl zero` to 0x31ff2b0, the routine the 6.5.30 entry names as `MakeCurrentRead`. The branch is direct, but t9 still matters: that routine sets gp from t9 at 0x31ff300 (`daddu gp,t9,v1`), so the slot supplies its entry address. The slot contents were not read. The frame pop is in the branch's delay slot. Code hash 896df58fcf4e.
